<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:

* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>

     1	"""Functions for subsampling datasets.
     2	
     3	"""
     4	import warnings
     5	
     6	import pandas as pd
     7	from pymbar.timeseries import (statisticalInefficiency,
     8	                               detectEquilibration,
     9	                               subsampleCorrelatedData, )
    10	from .. import pass_attrs
    11	
    12	def decorrelate_u_nk(df, method='dE', drop_duplicates=True,
    13	                     sort=True, remove_burnin=False, **kwargs):
    14	    """Subsample an u_nk DataFrame based on the selected method.
    15	
    16	    The method can be either 'all' (obtained as a sum over all energy
    17	    components) or 'dE'. In the latter case the energy differences
    18	    :math:`dE_{i,i+1}` (:math:`dE_{i,i-1}` for the last lambda) are used.  This
    19	    is a wrapper function around the function
    20	    :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.statistical_inefficiency` or
    21	    :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.equilibrium_detection`.
    22	
    23	    Parameters
    24	    ----------
    25	    df : DataFrame
    26	        DataFrame to be subsampled according to the selected method.
    27	    method : {'all', 'dE'}
    28	        Method for decorrelating the data.
    29	    drop_duplicates : bool
    30	        Drop the duplicated lines based on time.
    31	    sort : bool
    32	        Sort the Dataframe based on the time column.
    33	    remove_burnin : bool
    34	        Whether to perform equilibrium detection (``True``) or just do
    35	        statistical inefficiency (``False``).
    36	
    37	        .. versionadded:: 1.0.0
    38	
    39	    **kwargs :
    40	        Additional keyword arguments for
    41	        :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.statistical_inefficiency`
    42	        or :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.equilibrium_detection`.
    43	    Returns
    44	    -------
    45	    DataFrame
    46	        `df` subsampled according to selected `method`.
    47	    Note
    48	    ----
    49	    The default of ``True`` for  `drop_duplicates` and `sort` should result in robust decorrelation
    50	    but can lose data.
    51	
    52	
    53	    .. versionadded:: 0.6.0
    54	    .. versionchanged:: 1.0.0
    55	       Add the `remove_burnin` keyword to allow unequilibrated frames
    56	       to be removed. Rename `method` value 'dhdl_all' to 'all' and
    57	       deprecate the 'dhdl'.
    58	
    59	    """
    60	    kwargs['drop_duplicates'] = drop_duplicates
    61	    kwargs['sort'] = sort
    62	
    63	    series = u_nk2series(df, method)
    64	
    65	    if remove_burnin:
    66	        return equilibrium_detection(df, series, **kwargs)
    67	    else:
    68	        return statistical_inefficiency(df, series, **kwargs)
    69	
    70	def decorrelate_dhdl(df, drop_duplicates=True, sort=True,
    71	                     remove_burnin=False, **kwargs):
    72	    """Subsample a dhdl DataFrame.
    73	    This is a wrapper function around the function
    74	    :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.statistical_inefficiency` and
    75	    :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.equilibrium_detection`.
    76	
    77	    Parameters
    78	    ----------
    79	    df : DataFrame
    80	        DataFrame to subsample according to the selected method.
    81	    drop_duplicates : bool
    82	        Drop the duplicated lines based on time.
    83	    sort : bool
    84	        Sort the Dataframe based on the time column.
    85	    remove_burnin : bool
    86	        Whether to perform equilibrium detection (``True``) or just do
    87	        statistical inefficiency (``False``).
    88	
    89	        .. versionadded:: 1.0.0
    90	
    91	    **kwargs :
    92	        Additional keyword arguments for
    93	        :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.statistical_inefficiency`
    94	        or :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.equilibrium_detection`.
    95	
    96	    Returns
    97	    -------
    98	    DataFrame
    99	        `df` subsampled.
   100	
   101	    Note
   102	    ----
   103	    The default of ``True`` for `drop_duplicates` and `sort` should result in
   104	    robust decorrelation but can loose data.
   105	
   106	
   107	    .. versionadded:: 0.6.0
   108	    .. versionchanged:: 1.0.0
   109	       Add the `remove_burnin` keyword to allow unequilibrated frames to be
   110	       removed.
   111	
   112	    """
   113	
   114	    kwargs['drop_duplicates'] = drop_duplicates
   115	    kwargs['sort'] = sort
   116	
   117	    series = dhdl2series(df)
   118	
   119	    if remove_burnin:
   120	        return equilibrium_detection(df, series, **kwargs)
   121	    else:
   122	        return statistical_inefficiency(df, series, **kwargs)
   123	
   124	@pass_attrs
   125	def u_nk2series(df, method='dE'):
   126	    """Convert an u_nk DataFrame into a series based on the selected method
   127	    for subsampling.
   128	
   129	    The method can be either 'all' (obtained as a sum over all energy
   130	    components) or 'dE'. In the latter case the energy differences
   131	    :math:`dE_{i,i+1}` (:math:`dE_{i,i-1}` for the last lambda) are used.
   132	
   133	    Parameters
   134	    ----------
   135	    df : DataFrame
   136	        DataFrame to be converted according to the selected method.
   137	    method : {'all', 'dE'}
   138	        Method for converting the data.
   139	
   140	    Returns
   141	    -------
   142	    Series
   143	        `series` to be used as input for
   144	        :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.statistical_inefficiency`
   145	        or
   146	        :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.equilibrium_detection`.
   147	
   148	
   149	    .. versionadded:: 1.0.0
   150	
   151	    """
   152	
   153	    # deprecation: remove in 3.0.0
   154	    # (the deprecations should show up in the calling functions)
   155	    if method == 'dhdl':
   156	        warnings.warn("Method 'dhdl' has been deprecated, using 'dE' instead. "
   157	                      "'dhdl' will be removed in alchemlyb 3.0.0.",
   158	                      category=DeprecationWarning,
   159	                      stacklevel=2)
   160	        method = 'dE'
   161	    elif method == 'dhdl_all':
   162	        warnings.warn("Method 'dhdl_all' has been deprecated, using 'all' instead. "
   163	                      "'dhdl_all' will be removed in alchemlyb 3.0.0.",
   164	                      category=DeprecationWarning,
   165	                      stacklevel=2)
   166	        method = 'all'
   167	
   168	    # Check if the input is u_nk
   169	    try:
   170	        key = df.index.values[0][1:]
   171	        if len(key) == 1:
   172	            key = key[0]
   173	        df[key]
   174	    except KeyError:
   175	        raise ValueError('The input should be u_nk')
   176	
   177	    if method == 'all':
   178	        series = df.sum(axis=1)
   179	    elif method == 'dE':
   180	        # Using the same logic as alchemical-analysis
   181	        key = df.index.values[0][1:]
   182	        if len(key) == 1:
   183	            # For the case where there is a single lambda
   184	            index = df.columns.values.tolist().index(key[0])
   185	        else:
   186	            # For the case of more than 1 lambda
   187	            index = df.columns.values.tolist().index(key)
   188	            # for the state that is not the last state, take the state+1
   189	        if index + 1 < len(df.columns):
   190	            series = df.iloc[:, index + 1]
   191	            # for the state that is the last state, take the state-1
   192	        else:
   193	            series = df.iloc[:, index - 1]
   194	    else:
   195	        raise ValueError(
   196	            'Decorrelation method {} not found.'.format(method))
   197	    return series
   198	
   199	
   200	@pass_attrs
   201	def dhdl2series(df, method='all'):
   202	    """Convert a dhdl DataFrame to a series for subsampling.
   203	
   204	    The series is generated by summing over all energy components (axis 1 of
   205	    `df`), as for ``method='all'`` in :func:`u_nk2series`. Commonly, `df` only
   206	    contains a single energy component but in some cases (such as using a split
   207	    protocol in GROMACS), it can contain multiple columns for different energy
   208	    terms.
   209	
   210	    Parameters
   211	    ----------
   212	    df : DataFrame
   213	        DataFrame to subsample according to the selected method.
   214	    method : 'all'
   215	        Only 'all' is available; the keyword is provided for compatibility with
   216	        :func:`u_nk2series`.
   217	
   218	    Returns
   219	    -------
   220	    Series
   221	        `series` to be used as input for
   222	        :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.statistical_inefficiency`
   223	        or
   224	        :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.equilibrium_detection`.
   225	
   226	
   227	    .. versionadded:: 1.0.0
   228	
   229	    """
   230	    if method != "all":
   231	        raise ValueError("Only method='all' is supported for dhdl2series().")
   232	    series = df.sum(axis=1)
   233	    return series
   234	
   235	
   236	def _check_multiple_times(df):
   237	    if isinstance(df, pd.Series):
   238	        return df.sort_index(axis=0).reset_index('time', name='').duplicated('time').any()
   239	    else:
   240	        return df.sort_index(axis=0).reset_index('time').duplicated('time').any()
   241	
   242	
   243	def _check_sorted(df):
   244	    return df.reset_index(0)['time'].is_monotonic_increasing
   245	
   246	
   247	def _drop_duplicates(df, series=None):
   248	    """Drop the duplication in the ``df`` which could be Dataframe or
   249	    Series, if series is provided, format the series such that it has the
   250	    same length as ``df``.
   251	
   252	    Parameters
   253	    ----------
   254	    df : DataFrame or Series
   255	        DataFrame or Series where duplication will be dropped.
   256	    series : Series
   257	        series to be formatted in the same way as df.
   258	
   259	    Returns
   260	    -------
   261	    df : DataFrame or Series
   262	        Formatted DataFrame or Series.
   263	    series : Series
   264	        Formatted Series.
   265	    """
   266	    if isinstance(df, pd.Series):
   267	        # remove the duplicate based on time
   268	        drop_duplicates_series = df.reset_index('time', name=''). \
   269	            drop_duplicates('time')
   270	        # Rest the time index
   271	        lambda_names = ['time', ]
   272	        lambda_names.extend(drop_duplicates_series.index.names)
   273	        df = drop_duplicates_series.set_index('time', append=True). \
   274	            reorder_levels(lambda_names)
   275	    else:
   276	        # remove the duplicate based on time
   277	        drop_duplicates_df = df.reset_index('time').drop_duplicates('time')
   278	        # Rest the time index
   279	        lambda_names = ['time', ]
   280	        lambda_names.extend(drop_duplicates_df.index.names)
   281	        df = drop_duplicates_df.set_index('time', append=True). \
   282	            reorder_levels(lambda_names)
   283	
   284	    # Do the same withing with the series
   285	    if series is not None:
   286	        # remove the duplicate based on time
   287	        drop_duplicates_series = series.reset_index('time', name=''). \
   288	            drop_duplicates('time')
   289	        # Rest the time index
   290	        lambda_names = ['time', ]
   291	        lambda_names.extend(drop_duplicates_series.index.names)
   292	        series = drop_duplicates_series.set_index('time', append=True). \
   293	            reorder_levels(lambda_names)
   294	    return df, series
   295	
   296	def _sort_by_time(df, series=None):
   297	    """Sort the ``df`` by time which could be Dataframe or
   298	    Series, if series is provided, sort the series as well.
   299	
   300	    Parameters
   301	    ----------
   302	    df : DataFrame or Series
   303	        DataFrame or Series to be sorted by time.
   304	    series : Series
   305	        series to be sorted by time.
   306	
   307	    Returns
   308	    -------
   309	    df : DataFrame or Series
   310	        Formatted DataFrame or Series.
   311	    series : Series
   312	        Formatted Series.
   313	    """
   314	    df = df.sort_index(level='time')
   315	
   316	    if series is not None:
   317	        series = series.sort_index(level='time')
   318	    return df, series
   319	
   320	def _prepare_input(df, series, drop_duplicates, sort):
   321	    """Prepare and check the input to be used for statistical_inefficiency or equilibrium_detection.
   322	
   323	    Parameters
   324	    ----------
   325	    df : DataFrame or Series
   326	        DataFrame or Series to be Prepared and checked.
   327	    series : Series
   328	        series to be Prepared and checked.
   329	
   330	    Returns
   331	    -------
   332	    df : DataFrame or Series
   333	        Formatted DataFrame or Series.
   334	    series : Series
   335	        Formatted Series.
   336	    """
   337	    if _check_multiple_times(df):
   338	        if drop_duplicates:
   339	            df, series = _drop_duplicates(df, series)
   340	        else:
   341	            raise KeyError(
   342	                "Duplicate time values found; statistical inefficiency "
   343	                "only works on a single, contiguous, "
   344	                "and sorted timeseries.")
   345	
   346	    if not _check_sorted(df):
   347	        if sort:
   348	            df, series = _sort_by_time(df, series)
   349	        else:
   350	            raise KeyError(
   351	                "Statistical inefficiency only works as expected if "
   352	                "values are sorted by time, increasing.")
   353	
   354	    if series is not None:
   355	        if (len(series) != len(df) or
   356	                not all(
   357	                    series.reset_index()['time'] == df.reset_index()['time'])):
   358	            raise ValueError(
   359	                "series and data must be sampled at the same times")
   360	    return df, series
   361	
   362	def slicing(df, lower=None, upper=None, step=None, force=False):
   363	    """Subsample a DataFrame using simple slicing.
   364	
   365	    Parameters
   366	    ----------
   367	    df : DataFrame
   368	        DataFrame to subsample.
   369	    lower : float
   370	        Lower time to slice from.
   371	    upper : float
   372	        Upper time to slice to (inclusive).
   373	    step : int
   374	        Step between rows to slice by.
   375	    force : bool
   376	        Ignore checks that DataFrame is in proper form for expected behavior.
   377	
   378	    Returns
   379	    -------
   380	    DataFrame
   381	        `df` subsampled.
   382	
   383	    """
   384	    try:
   385	        df = df.loc[lower:upper:step]
   386	    except KeyError:
   387	        raise KeyError("DataFrame rows must be sorted by time, increasing.")
   388	
   389	    if not force and _check_multiple_times(df):
   390	        raise KeyError("Duplicate time values found; it's generally advised "
   391	                       "to use slicing on DataFrames with unique time values "
   392	                       "for each row. Use `force=True` to ignore this error.")
   393	
   394	    # drop any rows that have missing values
   395	    df = df.dropna()
   396	
   397	    return df
   398	
   399	
   400	def statistical_inefficiency(df, series=None, lower=None, upper=None,
   401	                             step=None, conservative=True,
   402	                             drop_duplicates=False, sort=False):
   403	    """Subsample a DataFrame based on the calculated statistical inefficiency
   404	    of a timeseries.
   405	
   406	    If `series` is ``None``, then this function will behave the same as
   407	    :func:`slicing`.
   408	
   409	    Parameters
   410	    ----------
   411	    df : DataFrame
   412	        DataFrame to subsample according statistical inefficiency of `series`.
   413	    series : Series
   414	        Series to use for calculating statistical inefficiency. If ``None``,
   415	        no statistical inefficiency-based subsampling will be performed.
   416	    lower : float
   417	        Lower bound to pre-slice `series` data from.
   418	    upper : float
   419	        Upper bound to pre-slice `series` to (inclusive).
   420	    step : int
   421	        Step between `series` items to pre-slice by.
   422	    conservative : bool
   423	        ``True`` use ``ceil(statistical_inefficiency)`` to slice the data in uniform
   424	        intervals (the default). ``False`` will sample at non-uniform intervals to
   425	        closely match the (fractional) statistical_inefficieny, as implemented
   426	        in :func:`pymbar.timeseries.subsampleCorrelatedData`.
   427	    drop_duplicates : bool
   428	        Drop the duplicated lines based on time.
   429	    sort : bool
   430	        Sort the Dataframe based on the time column.
   431	
   432	    Returns
   433	    -------
   434	    DataFrame
   435	        `df` subsampled according to subsampled `series`.
   436	
   437	    Warning
   438	    -------
   439	    The `series` and the data to be sliced, `df`, need to have the same number
   440	    of elements because the statistical inefficiency is calculated based on
   441	    the index of the series (and not an associated time). At the moment there is
   442	    no automatic conversion from a time to an index.
   443	
   444	    Note
   445	    ----
   446	    For a non-integer statistical ineffciency :math:`g`, the default value
   447	    ``conservative=True`` will provide _fewer_ data points than allowed by
   448	    :math:`g` and thus error estimates will be _higher_. For large numbers of
   449	    data points and converged free energies, the choice should not make a
   450	    difference. For small numbers of data points, ``conservative=True``
   451	    decreases a false sense of accuracy and is deemed the more careful and
   452	    conservative approach.
   453	
   454	    See Also
   455	    --------
   456	    pymbar.timeseries.statisticalInefficiency : detailed background
   457	    pymbar.timeseries.subsampleCorrelatedData : used for subsampling
   458	
   459	
   460	    .. versionchanged:: 0.2.0
   461	       The ``conservative`` keyword was added and the method is now using
   462	       ``pymbar.timeseries.statisticalInefficiency()``; previously, the statistical
   463	       inefficiency was _rounded_ (instead of ``ceil()``) and thus one could
   464	       end up with correlated data.
   465	
   466	    .. versionchanged:: 1.0.0
   467	       Fixed a bug that would effectively ignore the ``lower`` and ``step``
   468	       keywords when returning the subsampled DataFrame object. See
   469	       `issue #198 <https://github.com/alchemistry/alchemlyb/issues/198>`_ for
   470	       more details.
   471	
   472	    """
   473	    df, series = _prepare_input(df, series, drop_duplicates, sort)
   474	
   475	    if series is not None:
   476	        series = slicing(series, lower=lower, <response clipped><NOTE>Due to the max output limit, only part of this file has been shown to you. You should retry this tool after you have searched inside the file with `grep -n` in order to find the line numbers of what you are looking for.</NOTE>

   470	       more details.
   471	
   472	    """
   473	    df, series = _prepare_input(df, series, drop_duplicates, sort)
   474	
   475	    if series is not None:
   476	        series = slicing(series, lower=lower, upper=upper, step=step)
   477	        df = slicing(df, lower=lower, upper=upper, step=step)
   478	
   479	        # calculate statistical inefficiency of series (could use fft=True but needs test)
   480	        statinef = statisticalInefficiency(series, fast=False)
   481	
   482	        # use the subsampleCorrelatedData function to get the subsample index
   483	        indices = subsampleCorrelatedData(series, g=statinef,
   484	                                          conservative=conservative)
   485	        df = df.iloc[indices]
   486	    else:
   487	        df = slicing(df, lower=lower, upper=upper, step=step)
   488	
   489	    return df

   492	def equilibrium_detection(df, series=None, lower=None, upper=None, step=None,
   493	                          drop_duplicates=False, sort=False):
   494	    """Subsample a DataFrame using automated equilibrium detection on a timeseries.
   495	
   496	    This function uses the :mod:`pymbar` implementation of the *simple
   497	    automated equilibrium detection* algorithm in [Chodera2016]_.
   498	
   499	    If `series` is ``None``, then this function will behave the same as
   500	    :func:`slicing`.
   501	
   502	    Parameters
   503	    ----------
   504	    df : DataFrame
   505	        DataFrame to subsample according to equilibrium detection on `series`.
   506	    series : Series
   507	        Series to detect equilibration on. If ``None``, no equilibrium
   508	        detection-based subsampling will be performed.
   509	    lower : float
   510	        Lower bound to pre-slice `series` data from.
   511	    upper : float
   512	        Upper bound to pre-slice `series` to (inclusive).
   513	    step : int
   514	        Step between `series` items to pre-slice by.
   515	    drop_duplicates : bool
   516	        Drop the duplicated lines based on time.
   517	    sort : bool
   518	        Sort the Dataframe based on the time column.
   519	
   520	    Returns
   521	    -------
   522	    DataFrame
   523	        `df` subsampled according to subsampled `series`.
   524	
   525	    Notes
   526	    -----
   527	    Please cite [Chodera2016]_ when you use this function in published work.
   528	
   529	    See Also
   530	    --------
   531	    pymbar.timeseries.detectEquilibration : detailed background
   532	    pymbar.timeseries.subsampleCorrelatedData : used for subsampling
   533	
   534	
   535	    .. versionchanged:: 1.0.0
   536	        Add the drop_duplicates and sort keyword to unify the behaviour between
   537	        :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.statistical_inefficiency` or
   538	        :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.equilibrium_detection`.
   539	
   540	    """
   541	    df, series = _prepare_input(df, series, drop_duplicates, sort)
   542	
   543	    if series is not None:
   544	        series = slicing(series, lower=lower, upper=upper, step=step)
   545	        df = slicing(df, lower=lower, upper=upper, step=step)
   546	
   547	        # calculate statistical inefficiency of series, with equilibrium detection
   548	        t, statinef, Neff_max = detectEquilibration(series.values)
   549	
   550	        series_equil = series[t:]
   551	        df_equil = df[t:]
   552	
   553	        indices = subsampleCorrelatedData(series_equil, g=statinef)
   554	        df = df_equil.iloc[indices]
   555	    else:
   556	        df = slicing(df, lower=lower, upper=upper, step=step)
   557	
   558	    return df

     1	"""Parsers for extracting alchemical data from `NAMD <http://www.ks.uiuc.edu/Research/namd/>`_ output files.
     2	
     3	"""
     4	import pandas as pd
     5	import numpy as np
     6	from os.path import basename
     7	from re import split
     8	import logging
     9	from .util import anyopen
    10	from . import _init_attrs
    11	from ..postprocessors.units import R_kJmol, kJ2kcal
    12	
    13	logger = logging.getLogger("alchemlyb.parsers.NAMD")
    14	
    15	k_b = R_kJmol * kJ2kcal
    16	
    17	
    18	def _filename_sort_key(s):
    19	    """Key for natural-sorting filenames, ignoring the path.
    20	
    21	    This means that unlike with the standard Python sorted() function, "foo9" < "foo10".
    22	    """
    23	
    24	    return [int(t) if t.isdigit() else t.lower() for t in split(r'(\d+)', basename(s))]
    25	
    26	
    27	def _get_lambdas(fep_files):
    28	    """Retrieves all lambda values included in the FEP files provided.
    29	    
    30	    We have to do this in order to tolerate truncated and restarted fepout files.
    31	    The IDWS lambda is not present at the termination of the window, presumably
    32	    for backwards compatibility with ParseFEP and probably other things.
    33	
    34	    For a given lambda1, there can be only one lambda2 and at most one lambda_idws.
    35	
    36	    Parameters
    37	    ----------
    38	    fep_files: str or list of str
    39	        Path(s) to fepout files to extract data from.
    40	
    41	    Returns
    42	    -------
    43	    List of floats, or None if there is more than one lambda_idws for each lambda1.
    44	    """
    45	
    46	    lambda_fwd_map, lambda_bwd_map = {}, {}
    47	    is_ascending = set()
    48	    endpoint_windows = []
    49	
    50	    for fep_file in sorted(fep_files, key=_filename_sort_key):
    51	        with anyopen(fep_file, 'r') as f:
    52	            for line in f:
    53	                l = line.strip().split()
    54	
    55	                # We might not have a #NEW line so make the best guess
    56	                if l[0] == '#NEW':
    57	                    lambda1, lambda2 = float(l[6]), float(l[8])
    58	                    lambda_idws = float(l[10]) if 'LAMBDA_IDWS' in l else None
    59	                elif l[0] == '#Free':
    60	                    lambda1, lambda2, lambda_idws = float(l[7]), float(l[8]), None
    61	                else:
    62	                    # We only care about lines with lambda values. No need to
    63	                    # do all that other processing below for every line
    64	                    continue # pragma: no cover
    65	
    66	                # Keep track of whether the lambda values are increasing or decreasing, so we can return
    67	                # a sorted list of the lambdas in the correct order.
    68	                # If it changes during parsing of this set of fepout files, then we know something is wrong
    69	                
    70	                # Keep track of endpoints separately since in IDWS runs there must be one of opposite direction
    71	                if 0.0 in (lambda1, lambda2) or 1.0 in (lambda1, lambda2):
    72	                    endpoint_windows.append((lambda1, lambda2))
    73	                else:
    74	                    # If the lambdas are equal then this doesn't represent an ascending window
    75	                    if lambda2 != lambda1:
    76	                        is_ascending.add(lambda2 > lambda1)
    77	                    if lambda_idws is not None and lambda1 != lambda_idws:
    78	                        is_ascending.add(lambda1 > lambda_idws)
    79	
    80	                if len(is_ascending) > 1:
    81	                    raise ValueError(f'Lambda values change direction in {fep_file}, relative to the other files: {lambda1} -> {lambda2} (IDWS: {lambda_idws})')
    82	
    83	                # Make sure the lambda2 values are consistent
    84	                if lambda1 in lambda_fwd_map and lambda_fwd_map[lambda1] != lambda2:
    85	                    logger.error(f'fwd: lambda1 {lambda1} has lambda2 {lambda_fwd_map[lambda1]} in {fep_file} but it has already been {lambda2}')
    86	                    raise ValueError('More than one lambda2 value for a particular lambda1')
    87	
    88	                lambda_fwd_map[lambda1] = lambda2
    89	
    90	                # Make sure the lambda_idws values are consistent
    91	                if lambda_idws is not None:
    92	                    if lambda1 in lambda_bwd_map and lambda_bwd_map[lambda1] != lambda_idws:
    93	                        logger.error(f'bwd: lambda1 {lambda1} has lambda_idws {lambda_bwd_map[lambda1]} but it has already been {lambda_idws}')
    94	                        raise ValueError('More than one lambda_idws value for a particular lambda1')
    95	                    lambda_bwd_map[lambda1] = lambda_idws
    96	
    97	
    98	    is_ascending = next(iter(is_ascending))
    99	
   100	    all_lambdas = set()
   101	    all_lambdas.update(lambda_fwd_map.keys())
   102	    all_lambdas.update(lambda_fwd_map.values())
   103	    all_lambdas.update(lambda_bwd_map.keys())
   104	    all_lambdas.update(lambda_bwd_map.values())
   105	    return list(sorted(all_lambdas, reverse=not is_ascending))
   106	
   107	
   108	@_init_attrs
   109	def extract_u_nk(fep_files, T):
   110	    """Return reduced potentials `u_nk` from NAMD fepout file(s).
   111	
   112	    Parameters
   113	    ----------
   114	    fep_file : str or list of str
   115	        Path to fepout file(s) to extract data from. These are sorted by filename,
   116	        not including the path, prior to processing, using natural-sort. This way,
   117	        filenames including numbers without leading zeros are handled intuitively.
   118	
   119	        Windows may be split across files, or more than one window may be present
   120	        in a given file. Windows without footer lines (which may be in a different
   121	        file than the respective header lines) will raise an error. This means that
   122	        while windows may have been interrupted and restarted, they must be
   123	        complete. Lambda values are expected to increase or decrease monotonically,
   124	        and match between header and footer of each window.
   125	
   126	    T : float
   127	        Temperature in Kelvin at which the simulation was sampled.
   128	
   129	    Returns
   130	    -------
   131	    u_nk : DataFrame
   132	        Potential energy for each alchemical state (k) for each frame (n).
   133	
   134	    Note
   135	    ----
   136	    If the number of forward and backward samples in a given window are different,
   137	    the extra sample(s) will be discarded. This is typically zero or one sample.
   138	
   139	
   140	    .. versionchanged:: 0.5.0
   141	        The :mod:`scipy.constants` is used for parsers instead of
   142	        the constants used by the corresponding MD engine.
   143	
   144	    .. versionchanged:: 0.6.0
   145	        Support for Interleaved Double-Wide Sampling files added, with various
   146	        robustness checks.
   147	
   148	        `fep_files` can now be a list of filenames.
   149	    """
   150	    beta = 1/(k_b * T)
   151	
   152	    # lists to get times and work values of each window
   153	    win_ts = []
   154	    win_de = []
   155	    win_ts_back = []
   156	    win_de_back = []
   157	
   158	    # create dataframe for results
   159	    u_nk = pd.DataFrame(columns=['time','fep-lambda'])
   160	
   161	    # boolean flag to parse data after equil time
   162	    parsing = False
   163	
   164	    if type(fep_files) is str:
   165	        fep_files = [fep_files]
   166	
   167	    # Extract the lambda values only from the fepouts
   168	    all_lambdas = _get_lambdas(fep_files)
   169	    # open and get data from fep file.
   170	    # We sort the list of fep files in case some of them represent restarted windows.
   171	    # The assumption is that they make sense in lexicographic order.
   172	    # We keep track of which lambda window we're in, but since it can span multiple files,
   173	    # only reset these variables here and after the end of each window
   174	    lambda1_at_start, lambda2_at_start, lambda_idws_at_start = None, None, None
   175	
   176	    for fep_file in sorted(fep_files, key=_filename_sort_key):
   177	        # Note we have not set parsing=False because we could be continuing one window across
   178	        # more than one fepout file
   179	        with anyopen(fep_file, 'r') as f:
   180	            has_idws = False
   181	            for line in f:
   182	                l = line.strip().split()
   183	                # We don't know if IDWS was enabled just from the #Free line, and we might not have
   184	                # a #NEW line in this file, so we have to check for the existence of FepE_back lines
   185	                # We rely on short-circuit evaluation to avoid the string comparison most of the time
   186	                if has_idws is False and l[0] == 'FepE_back:':
   187	                    has_idws = True
   188	
   189	                # New window, get IDWS lambda if any
   190	                # We keep track of lambdas from the #NEW line and if they disagree with the #Free line
   191	                # within the same file, then complain. This can happen if truncated fepout files
   192	                # are presented in the wrong order.
   193	                if l[0] == '#NEW':
   194	                    if parsing:
   195	                        logger.error(f'Window with lambda1: {lambda1_at_start} lambda2: {lambda2_at_start} lambda_idws: {lambda_idws_at_start} appears truncated')
   196	                        logger.error(f'because a new window was encountered in {fep_file} before the previous one finished.')
   197	                        raise ValueError('New window begun after truncated window')
   198	
   199	                    lambda1_at_start, lambda2_at_start = float(l[6]), float(l[8])
   200	                    lambda_idws_at_start = float(l[10]) if 'LAMBDA_IDWS' in l else None
   201	                    has_idws = True if lambda_idws_at_start is not None else False
   202	
   203	                # this line marks end of window; dump data into dataframe
   204	                if l[0] == '#Free':
   205	                    # extract lambda values for finished window
   206	                    # lambda1 = sampling lambda (row), lambda2 = comparison lambda (col)
   207	                    lambda1 = float(l[7])
   208	                    lambda2 = float(l[8])
   209	
   210	                    # If the lambdas are not what we thought they would be, raise an exception to ensure the calculation
   211	                    # fails. This can happen if fepouts where one window spans multiple fepouts are processed out of order
   212	                    # NB: There is no way to tell if lambda_idws changed because it isn't in the '#Free' line that ends a window
   213	                    if lambda1_at_start is not None \
   214	                        and (lambda1, lambda2) != (lambda1_at_start, lambda2_at_start):
   215	                        logger.error(f"Lambdas changed unexpectedly while processing {fep_file}")
   216	                        logger.error(f"l1, l2: {lambda1_at_start}, {lambda2_at_start} changed to {lambda1}, {lambda2}")
   217	                        logger.error(line)
   218	                        raise ValueError("Inconsistent lambda values within the same window")
   219	
   220	                    # As we are at the end of a window, convert last window's work and times values to np arrays
   221	                    # (with energy unit kT since they were kcal/mol in the fepouts)
   222	                    win_de_arr = beta * np.asarray(win_de) # dE values
   223	                    win_ts_arr = np.asarray(win_ts) # timesteps
   224	
   225	                    # This handles the special case where there are IDWS energies but no lambda_idws value in the
   226	                    # current .fepout file. This can happen when the NAMD firsttimestep is not 0, because NAMD only emits
   227	                    # the '#NEW' line on timestep 0 for some reason. Perhaps the user ran minimize before dynamics,
   228	                    # or this is a restarted run.
   229	                    # We infer lambda_idws_at_start if it wasn't explictly included in this fepout.
   230	                    # If lambdas are in ascending order, choose the one before it all_lambdas, and if descending, choose
   231	                    # the one after. This happens "automatically" because the lambdas were returned already sorted
   232	                    # in the correct direction by _get_lambdas().
   233	                    # The "else" case is handled by the rest of this block, by default.
   234	                    if has_idws and lambda_idws_at_start is None:
   235	                        l1_idx = all_lambdas.index(lambda1)
   236	                        # Test for the highly pathological case where the first window is both incomplete and has IDWS
   237	                        # data but no lambda_idws value.
   238	                        if l1_idx == 0:
   239	                            raise ValueError(f'IDWS data present in first window but lambda_idws not included; no way to infer the correct lambda_idws')
   240	                        lambda_idws_at_start = all_lambdas[l1_idx - 1]
   241	                        logger.warning(f'Warning: {fep_file} has IDWS data but lambda_idws not included.')
   242	                        logger.warning(f'         lambda1 = {lambda1}, lambda2 = {lambda2}; inferring lambda_idws to be {lambda_idws_at_start}')
   243	
   244	                    if lambda_idws_at_start is not None:
   245	                        # Mimic classic DWS data
   246	                        # Arbitrarily match up fwd and bwd comparison energies on the same times
   247	                        # truncate extra samples from whichever array is longer
   248	                        win_de_back_arr = beta * np.asarray(win_de_back)
   249	                        n = min(len(win_de_back_arr), len(win_de_arr))
   250	
   251	                        tempDF = pd.DataFrame({
   252	                            'time': win_ts_arr[:n],
   253	                            'fep-lambda': np.full(n,lambda1),
   254	                            lambda1: 0,
   255	                            lambda2: win_de_arr[:n],
   256	                            lambda_idws_at_start: win_de_back_arr[:n]})
   257	                        # print(f"{fep_file}: IDWS window {lambda1} {lambda2} {lambda_idws_at_start}")
   258	                    else:
   259	                        # print(f"{fep_file}: Forward-only window {lambda1} {lambda2}")
   260	                        # create dataframe of times and work values
   261	                        # this window's data goes in row LAMBDA1 and column LAMBDA2
   262	                        tempDF = pd.DataFrame({
   263	                            'time': win_ts_arr,
   264	                            'fep-lambda': np.full(len(win_de_arr), lambda1),
   265	                            lambda1: 0,
   266	                            lambda2: win_de_arr})
   267	
   268	                    # join the new window's df to existing df
   269	                    u_nk = pd.concat([u_nk, tempDF], sort=False)
   270	
   271	                    # reset values for next window of fepout file
   272	                    win_de = []
   273	                    win_ts = []
   274	                    win_de_back = []
   275	                    win_ts_back = []
   276	                    parsing = False
   277	                    has_idws = False
   278	                    lambda1_at_start, lambda2_at_start, lambda_idws_at_start = None, None, None
   279	
   280	                # append work value from 'dE' column of fepout file
   281	                if parsing:
   282	                    if l[0] == 'FepEnergy:':
   283	                        win_de.append(float(l[6]))
   284	                        win_ts.append(float(l[1]))
   285	                    elif l[0] == 'FepE_back:':
   286	                        win_de_back.append(float(l[6]))
   287	                        win_ts_back.append(float(l[1]))
   288	
   289	                # Turn parsing on after line 'STARTING COLLECTION OF ENSEMBLE AVERAGE'
   290	                if '#STARTING' in l:
   291	                    parsing = True
   292	
   293	    if len(win_de) != 0 or len(win_de_back) != 0: # pragma: no cover
   294	        logger.warning('Trailing data without footer line (\"#Free energy...\"). Interrupted run?')
   295	        raise ValueError('Last window is truncated')
   296	
   297	
   298	    if lambda2 in (0.0, 1.0):
   299	        # this excludes the IDWS case where a dataframe already exists for both endpoints
   300	        # create last dataframe for fep-lambda at last LAMBDA2
   301	        tempDF = pd.DataFrame({
   302	            'time': win_ts_arr,
   303	            'fep-lambda': lambda2})
   304	        u_nk = pd.concat([u_nk, tempDF], sort=True)
   305	
   306	    u_nk.set_index(['time','fep-lambda'], inplace=True)
   307	
   308	    return u_nk
   309

    65	
    66	def _check_data_is_outside_bounds(data, lower, upper):
    67	    """
    68	    Helper function to make sure that `data` has entries that are
    69	    below the `lower` bound, and above the `upper` bound.
    70	    This is used by slicing tests to make sure that the data
    71	    provided is appropriate for the tests.
    72	    """
    73	    assert any(data.reset_index()['time'] < lower)
    74	    assert any(data.reset_index()['time'] > upper)

    77	class TestSlicing:
    78	    """Test slicing functionality.
    79	
    80	    """
    81	    def slicer(self, *args, **kwargs):
    82	        return slicing(*args, **kwargs)
    83	
    84	    @pytest.mark.parametrize(('data', 'size'), [(gmx_benzene_dHdl(), 661),
    85	                                                (gmx_benzene_u_nk(), 661)])
    86	    def test_basic_slicing(self, data, size):
    87	        assert len(self.slicer(data, lower=1000, upper=34000, step=5)) == size
    88	
    89	    @pytest.mark.parametrize(('dataloader', 'lower', 'upper'),
    90	                             [
    91	                                 ('gmx_benzene_dHdl_fixture', 1000, 34000),
    92	                                 ('gmx_benzene_u_nk_fixture', 1000, 34000),
    93	                             ])
    94	    def test_data_is_unchanged(self, dataloader, lower, upper, request):
    95	        """
    96	        Test that slicing does not change the underlying data
    97	        """
    98	        # Load data
    99	        data = request.getfixturevalue(dataloader)
   100	        # Check that the input data is appropriate for the test
   101	        _check_data_is_outside_bounds(data, lower, upper)
   102	
   103	        # Slice data, and test that we didn't change the input data
   104	        original_length = len(data)
   105	        sliced = self.slicer(data,
   106	                             lower=lower,
   107	                             upper=upper,
   108	                             step=5)
   109	        assert len(data) == original_length

   111	    @pytest.mark.parametrize(('dataloader', 'lower', 'upper'),
   112	                             [
   113	                                 ('gmx_benzene_dHdl_fixture', 1000, 34000),
   114	                                 ('gmx_benzene_u_nk_fixture', 1000, 34000),
   115	                             ])
   116	    def test_lower_and_upper_bound(self, dataloader, lower, upper, request):
   117	        """
   118	        Test that the lower and upper time is respected
   119	        """
   120	        # Load data
   121	        data = request.getfixturevalue(dataloader)
   122	        # Check that the input data is appropriate for the test
   123	        _check_data_is_outside_bounds(data, lower, upper)
   124	
   125	        # Slice data, and test that we don't observe times outside
   126	        # the prescribed range
   127	        sliced = self.slicer(data,
   128	                             lower=lower,
   129	                             upper=upper,
   130	                             step=5)
   131	        assert all(sliced.reset_index()['time'] >= lower)
   132	        assert all(sliced.reset_index()['time'] <= upper)

   228	class CorrelatedPreprocessors:
   229	
   230	    @pytest.mark.parametrize(('data', 'size'), [(gmx_benzene_dHdl(), 4001),
   231	                                                (gmx_benzene_u_nk(), 4001)])
   232	    def test_subsampling(self, data, size):
   233	        """Basic test for execution; resulting size of dataset sensitive to
   234	        machine and depends on algorithm.
   235	        """
   236	        assert len(self.slicer(data, series=data.iloc[:, 0])) <= size

   238	    @pytest.mark.parametrize('data', [gmx_benzene_dHdl(),
   239	                                      gmx_benzene_u_nk()])
   240	    def test_no_series(self, data):
   241	        """Check that we get the same result as simple slicing with no Series.
   242	
   243	        """
   244	        df_sub = self.slicer(data, lower=200, upper=5000, step=2)
   245	        df_sliced = slicing(data, lower=200, upper=5000, step=2)
   246	
   247	        assert np.all((df_sub == df_sliced))

   252	    def slicer(self, *args, **kwargs):
   253	        return statistical_inefficiency(*args, **kwargs)

   255	    @pytest.mark.parametrize(('conservative', 'data', 'size'),
   256	                             [
   257	                                 (True, gmx_benzene_dHdl(), 2001),  # 0.00:  g = 1.0559445620585415
   258	                                 (True, gmx_benzene_u_nk(), 2001),  # 'fep': g = 1.0560203916559594
   259	                                 (False, gmx_benzene_dHdl(), 3789),
   260	                                 (False, gmx_benzene_u_nk(), 3571),
   261	                             ])
   262	    def test_conservative(self, data, size, conservative):
   263	        sliced = self.slicer(data, series=data.iloc[:, 0], conservative=conservative)
   264	        # results can vary slightly with different machines
   265	        # so possibly do
   266	        # delta = 10
   267	        # assert size - delta < len(sliced) < size + delta
   268	        assert len(sliced) == size

   279	    @pytest.mark.parametrize(('dataloader', 'lower', 'upper'),
   280	                             [
   281	                                 ('gmx_benzene_dHdl_fixture', 1000, 34000),

   283	                             ])
   284	    @pytest.mark.parametrize('use_series', [True, False])
   285	    @pytest.mark.parametrize('conservative', [True, False])
   286	    def test_data_is_unchanged(
   287	            self, dataloader, use_series, lower, upper, conservative, request
   288	    ):
   289	        """
   290	        Test that using statistical_inefficiency does not change the underlying data
   291	
   292	        statistical_inefficiency is equivalent to slicing it its `series` parameter
   293	        is not set. If the `series` parameter is set, additional inefficiency
   294	        calculations are performed. We want to test both behaviors. The behavior
   295	        is toggled using the `use_series` argument.
   296	        """
   297	        # Load data
   298	        data = request.getfixturevalue(dataloader)
   299	        # Check that the input data is appropriate for the test
   300	        _check_data_is_outside_bounds(data, lower, upper)
   301	
   302	        # Define subsampling series if required
   303	        series = data.sum(axis=1) if use_series else None
   304	
   305	        # Slice data, and test that we didn't change the input data
   306	        original_length = len(data)
   307	        self.slicer(data,
   308	                    series=series,
   309	                    lower=lower,
   310	                    upper=upper,
   311	                    step=5,
   312	                    conservative=conservative)
   313	        assert len(data) == original_length

    58	
    59	def _corrupt_fepout(fepout_in, params, tmp_path):
    60	    """Corrupts specific lines in a fepout file according to each line's prefix,
    61	    using caller-supplied functions.
    62	
    63	    Parameters
    64	    ----------
    65	    fepout_in: str
    66	        Path to fepout file to be modified. This file will not be overwritten.
    67	    
    68	    params: list of tuples
    69	        For each tuple, the first element must be a str that will be passed to
    70	        startswith() to identify the line(s) to modify (e.g. "#NEW"). The
    71	        second element must be a function that accepts a list of strs which is
    72	        the output of running split() on the identified line and returns
    73	        a modified list of tokens that will be reassembled into the line to be
    74	        output.
    75	
    76	        The function may return None if this line should not be written. This
    77	        can be used to delete lines from the fepout.
    78	
    79	    Returns
    80	    -------
    81	    The name of a temporary file which pytest will unlink.
    82	    """
    83	
    84	    fepout_out = tmp_path / basename(fepout_in)
    85	    with bz2.open(fepout_out, 'wt') as f_out:
    86	        with bz2.open(fepout_in, 'rt') as f_in:
    87	            for line in f_in:
    88	                for prefix, func in params:
    89	                    if line.startswith(prefix):
    90	                        tokens_out = func(line.split())
    91	                        line = ' '.join(tokens_out) + '\n' if tokens_out is not None else None
    92	                if line is not None:
    93	                    f_out.write(line)
    94	    return str(fepout_out)
    95

    97	@pytest.fixture
    98	def restarted_dataset_inconsistent(restarted_dataset, tmp_path):
    99	    """Returns intentionally messed up dataset where lambda1 and lambda2 at start and end of
   100	    a window are different."""
   101	
   102	    filenames = sorted(restarted_dataset['data']['both'])
   103	
   104	    changed = False
   105	    def func_free_line(l):
   106	        nonlocal changed
   107	        if float(l[7]) >= 0.7 and float(l[7]) < 0.9:
   108	            l[7] = str(float(l[7]) + 0.0001)
   109	            changed = True
   110	        return l
   111	
   112	    for i in range(len(filenames)):
   113	        filenames[i] = _corrupt_fepout(filenames[i], [('#Free', func_free_line)], tmp_path)
   114	        # Only actually modify one window so we don't trigger the wrong exception
   115	        if changed is True:
   116	            break
   117	
   118	    # Don't directly modify the glob object
   119	    restarted_dataset['data']['both'] = filenames
   120	
   121	    return restarted_dataset

   124	@pytest.fixture
   125	def restarted_dataset_idws_without_lambda_idws(restarted_dataset, tmp_path):
   126	    """Returns intentionally messed up dataset where the first window has IDWS data
   127	    but no lambda_idws.
   128	    """
   129	
   130	    # First window won't have any IDWS data so we just drop all its files and fudge the lambdas
   131	    # in the next window to include 0.0 or 1.0 (as appropriate) so we still have a nominally complete calculation
   132	    
   133	    filenames = [x for x in sorted(restarted_dataset['data']['both']) if search('000[a-z]?.fepout', x) is None]
   134	
   135	    def func_new_line(l):
   136	        if float(l[LAMBDA1_IDX_NEW]) > 0.5: # 1->0 (reversed) calculation
   137	            l[LAMBDA1_IDX_NEW] == '1.0'
   138	        else: # regular 0->1 calculation
   139	            l[LAMBDA1_IDX_NEW] = '0.0'
   140	        # Drop the lambda_idws
   141	        return l[:9]
   142	    
   143	    def func_free_line(l):
   144	        if float(l[LAMBDA1_IDX_FREE]) > 0.5: # 1->0 (reversed) calculation
   145	            l[LAMBDA1_IDX_FREE] == '1.0'
   146	        else: # regular 0->1 calculation
   147	            l[LAMBDA1_IDX_FREE] = '0.0'
   148	        return l
   149	        
   150	    filenames[0] = _corrupt_fepout(filenames[0], [('#NEW', func_new_line), ('#Free', func_free_line)], tmp_path)
   151	    restarted_dataset['data']['both'] = filenames
   152	    return restarted_dataset

   156	def restarted_dataset_toomany_lambda2(restarted_dataset, tmp_path):
   157	    """Returns intentionally messed up dataset, where there are too many lambda2 values for a

   160	    filenames = sorted(restarted_dataset['data']['both'])
   161	
   162	    # For the same l1 and lidws we retain old lambda2 values thus ensuring a collision
   163	    # Also, don't make a window where lambda1 >= lambda2 because this will trigger the
   164	    # "direction changed" exception instead
   165	    def func_new_line(l):
   166	        if float(l[LAMBDA2_IDX_NEW]) <= 0.2:
   167	            return l
   168	        l[LAMBDA1_IDX_NEW] = '0.2'
   169	        if len(l) > 9 and l[9] == 'LAMBDA_IDWS':
   170	            l[LAMBDA_IDWS_IDX_NEW] = '0.1'
   171	        return l
   172	
   173	    def func_free_line(l):
   174	        if float(l[LAMBDA2_IDX_FREE]) <= 0.2:
   175	            return l
   176	        l[LAMBDA1_IDX_FREE] = '0.2'
   177	        return l
   178	
   179	    for i in range(len(filenames)):
   180	        filenames[i] = \
   181	            _corrupt_fepout(filenames[i], [('#NEW', func_new_line), ('#Free', func_free_line)], tmp_path)
   182	
   183	    restarted_dataset['data']['both'] = filenames
   184	    return restarted_dataset
   185	
   186	
   187	@pytest.fixture
   188	def restarted_dataset_toomany_lambda_idws(restarted_dataset, tmp_path):
   189	    """Returns intentionally messed up dataset, where there are too many lambda2 values for a
   190	    given lambda1."""
   191	
   192	    filenames = sorted(restarted_dataset['data']['both'])
   193	
   194	    # For the same lambda1 and lambda2 we retain the first set of lambda1/lambda2 values
   195	    # and replicate them across all windows thus ensuring that there will be more than
   196	    # one lambda_idws value for a given lambda1 and lambda2
   197	    this_lambda1, this_lambda2 = None, None
   198	
   199	    def func_new_line(l):
   200	        nonlocal this_lambda1, this_lambda2
   201	        
   202	        if this_lambda1 is None:
   203	            this_lambda1, this_lambda2 = l[LAMBDA1_IDX_NEW], l[LAMBDA2_IDX_NEW]
   204	        # Ensure that changing these lambda values won't cause a reversal in direction and trigger
   205	        # an exception we're not trying to test here
   206	        if len(l) > 9 and float(l[LAMBDA_IDWS_IDX_NEW]) < 0.5:
   207	            l[LAMBDA1_IDX_NEW], l[LAMBDA2_IDX_NEW] = this_lambda1, this_lambda2
   208	        return l
   209	
   210	    def func_free_line(l):
   211	        l[LAMBDA1_IDX_FREE], l[LAMBDA2_IDX_FREE] = this_lambda1, this_lambda2
   212	        return l
   213	
   214	    for i in range(len(filenames)):
   215	        filenames[i] = _corrupt_fepout(filenames[i], [('#NEW', func_new_line)], tmp_path)
   216	
   217	    restarted_dataset['data']['both'] = filenames
   218	    return restarted_dataset
   219	
   220	
   221	@pytest.fixture
   222	def restarted_dataset_direction_changed(restarted_dataset, tmp_path):
   223	    """Returns intentionally messed up dataset, with one window where the lambda values are reversed."""
   224	
   225	    filenames = sorted(restarted_dataset['data']['both'])
   226	
   227	    def func_new_line(l):
   228	        l[6], l[8], l[10] = l[10], l[8], l[6]
   229	        return l
   230	
   231	    def func_free_line(l):
   232	        l[7], l[8] = l[8], l[7]
   233	        return l
   234	    
   235	    # Reverse the direction of lambdas for this window
   236	    idx_to_corrupt = filenames.index(sorted(filenames)[-3])
   237	    fname1 = _corrupt_fepout(filenames[idx_to_corrupt], [('#NEW', func_new_line), ('#Free', func_free_line)], tmp_path)
   238	    filenames[idx_to_corrupt] = fname1
   239	    restarted_dataset['data']['both'] = filenames
   240	    return restarted_dataset
   241	
   242	
   243	@pytest.fixture

   247	    filenames = sorted(restarted_dataset['data']['both'])

   249	    def func_free_line(l):
   250	        return None
   251	
   252	    for i in range(len(filenames)):

   273	def test_u_nk_restarted():
   274	    """Test that u_nk has the correct form when extracted from an IDWS
   275	    FEP run that includes terminations and restarts.
   276	    """
   277	    filenames = load_restarted()['data']['both']

   284	def test_u_nk_restarted_missing_window_header(tmp_path):
   285	    """Test that u_nk has the correct form when a #NEW line is missing from the restarted dataset
   286	    and the parser has to infer lambda_idws for that window."""
   287	    filenames = sorted(load_restarted()['data']['both'])
   288	    # Remove "#NEW" line
   289	    filenames[4] = _corrupt_fepout(filenames[4], [('#NEW', lambda l: None),], tmp_path)
   290	
   291	    u_nk = extract_u_nk(filenames, T=300)
   292

   294	    assert u_nk.shape == (30061, 11)

1. The issue is about NAMD decorrelation and equilibration detection failing in v0.7 and 1.0, but working in v0.6.0
2. The user mentions that the problem is likely in preprocessing.py (which maps to subsampling.py in the current codebase)
3. The user also mentions that decorrelation should work on the entire dataframe but currently users have to separate by fep-lambda
4. When not separated, decorrelate_u_nk returns just fep-lambda=0

Let me think about what changed between versions. Looking at the CHANGES file, I see significant changes in version 1.0.0:
- Refactor the subsampling module to unify the behaviour of equilibrium_detection and statistical_inefficiency (PR #218).
- Add u_nk2series and dhdl2series to convert u_nk and dHdl to series (PR #218).
- Add remove_burnin keyword to decorrelate_u_nk and decorrelate_dhdl (PR #218).

And in version 0.7.0:
- Fixed subsampling in statistical_inefficiency when lower or step keywords are used (Issue #198, PR #199)

The key insight is that the introduction of u_nk2series in 1.0.0 might be causing the issue. Let me look at how u_nk2series works and see if it properly handles multi-indexed dataframes with different fep-lambda values.

Looking at the u_nk2series function in subsampling.py, I can see that it has logic to handle the index values:
- Line 170: `key = df.index.values[0][1:]` - this gets the lambda values from the first row
- Lines 181-193: Logic to select which column to use based on the lambda index

The problem might be that when we have a dataframe with multiple fep-lambda values, the u_nk2series function only looks at the first row's lambda values to determine which column to use. This would mean that for a mixed dataframe with multiple fep-lambda values, it would consistently pick the same column (probably corresponding to fep-lambda=0) instead of properly handling each fep-lambda separately.

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I can see the issue! The problem is in the `u_nk2series` function. Let me trace through what happens:

1. Line 170: `key = df.index.values[0][1:]` - This gets the lambda values from the FIRST row only
2. Lines 181-193: The function determines which column to use based ONLY on the first row's lambda values

This means that when we have a DataFrame with multiple fep-lambda values (like NAMD data), the function will always use the column selection logic based on the first row, regardless of what fep-lambda each subsequent row belongs to.

For example, if the first row has fep-lambda=0, it will consistently pick the same column for ALL rows, even those that belong to different fep-lambda values. This explains why "decorrelate_u_nk returns just fep-lambda=0" - it's because the series is computed using the wrong column for all rows except those that actually belong to fep-lambda=0.

Author: Zhiyi Wu <zhiyi.wu@bioch.ox.ac.uk>
Date:   Mon Sep 12 21:44:38 2022 +0100

    Refactor the subsampling module to unify the behaviour of equilibrium_detection and statistical_inefficiency (#218)

    * Add the keyword remove_burnin to the high-level function decorrelate_u_nk and decorrelate_dhdl to use
       equilibrium_detection instead of statistical_inefficiency.
    * Extract the function u_nk2series and dhdl2series to make series from dataframe
    * Extract the function _drop_duplicates, _sort_by_time and _prepare_input to clean the data before passing to
       equilibrium_detection and statistical_inefficiency
    * Unify the behaviour of equilibrium_detection and statistical_inefficiency in terms of preprocessing and
       checking the data.
    * add docs for new functions
    * update tests and add tests
    * update CHANGELOG for 1.0

    Co-authored-by: Zhiyi Wu <zwu@exscientia.co.uk>
    Co-authored-by: Oliver Beckstein <orbeckst@gmail.com>

"""
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from pymbar.timeseries import (statisticalInefficiency,
                               detectEquilibration,
                               subsampleCorrelatedData, )

def decorrelate_u_nk(df, method='dhdl', drop_duplicates=True,
                     sort=True, **kwargs):
    """Subsample a u_nk DataFrame based on the selected method.

    The method can be either 'dhdl_all' (obtained as a sum over all energy
    components) or 'dhdl' (obtained as the energy components that are
    changing; default) or 'dE'. In the latter case the energy differences
    dE_{i,i+1} (dE_{i,i-1} for the last lambda) are used.

    This is a wrapper function around the function
    :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.statistical_inefficiency`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    df : DataFrame
        DataFrame to be subsampled according to the selected method.
    method : {'dhdl', 'dhdl_all', 'dE'}
        Method for decorrelating the data.
    drop_duplicates : bool
        Drop the duplicated lines based on time.
    sort : bool
        Sort the Dataframe based on the time column.
    **kwargs :
        Additional keyword arguments for
        :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.statistical_inefficiency`.

    Returns
    -------
    DataFrame
        `df` subsampled according to selected `method`.

    Note
    ----
    The default of ``True`` for  `drop_duplicates` and `sort` should result in robust decorrelation
    but can loose data.

    .. versionadded:: 0.6.0
    """
    kwargs['drop_duplicates'] = drop_duplicates
    kwargs['sort'] = sort

    # Check if the input is u_nk
    try:
        key = df.index.values[0][1:]
        if len(key) == 1:
            key = key[0]
        df[key]
    except KeyError:
        raise ValueError('The input should be u_nk')

    if method == 'dhdl':
        # Find the current column index
        # Select the first row and remove the first column (Time)
        key = df.index.values[0][1:]
        if len(key) > 1:
            # Multiple keys
            series = df[key]
        else:
            # Single key
            series = df[key[0]]
    elif method == 'dhdl_all':
        series = df.sum(axis=1)
    elif method == 'dE':
        # Using the same logic as alchemical-analysis
        key = df.index.values[0][1:]
        if len(key) == 1:
            # For the case where there is a single lambda
            index = df.columns.values.tolist().index(key[0])
        else:
            # For the case of more than 1 lambda
            index = df.columns.values.tolist().index(key)
            # for the state that is not the last state, take the state+1
        if index + 1 < len(df.columns):
            series = df.iloc[:, index + 1]
            # for the state that is the last state, take the state-1
        else:
            series = df.iloc[:, index - 1]
    else: # pragma: no cover
        raise ValueError(
            'Decorrelation method {} not found.'.format(method))
    return statistical_inefficiency(df, series, **kwargs)

def decorrelate_dhdl(df, drop_duplicates=True, sort=True, **kwargs):
    """Subsample a dhdl DataFrame.

    This is a wrapper function around the function
    :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.statistical_inefficiency`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    df : DataFrame
[The command completed with exit code 0.]

[Python interpreter: /opt/conda/envs/testbed/bin/python]

        # Using the same logic as alchemical-analysis

        else:

        else:
            series = df.iloc[:, index - 1]
    else: # pragma: no cover
        raise ValueError(
            'Decorrelation method {} not found.'.format(method))
    return statistical_inefficiency(df, series, **kwargs)

def decorrelate_dhdl(df, drop_duplicates=True, sort=True, **kwargs):
    """Subsample a dhdl DataFrame.

    This is a wrapper function around the function
    :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.statistical_inefficiency`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    df : DataFrame
        DataFrame to subsample according to the selected method.
    drop_duplicates : bool
        Drop the duplicated lines based on time.
    sort : bool
        Sort the Dataframe based on the time column.
    **kwargs :
        Additional keyword arguments for
        :func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.statistical_inefficiency`.

    Returns
    -------
    DataFrame
        `df` subsampled.

    Note
    ----
    The default of ``True`` for  `drop_duplicates` and `sort` should result in robust decorrelation
    but can loose data.


    .. versionadded:: 0.6.0
    """

     1	.. _subsampling:
     2	
     3	Subsampling
     4	===========
     5	
     6	.. automodule:: alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling
     7	
     8	The functions featured in this module can be used to easily subsample either
     9	:ref:`dHdl <dHdl>` or :ref:`u_nk <u_nk>` datasets to give less correlated
    10	timeseries.
    11	
    12	High-level functions
    13	--------------------
    14	Two high-level functions
    15	:func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.decorrelate_u_nk` and
    16	:func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.decorrelate_dhdl` can be used to
    17	preprocess the :ref:`dHdl <dHdl>` or :ref:`u_nk <u_nk>` in an automatic
    18	fashion. The following code removes an initial "burnin" period and
    19	decorrelates the data. ::
    20	
    21	    >>> from alchemlyb.parsing.gmx import extract_u_nk, extract_dHdl
    22	    >>> from alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling import (decorrelate_u_nk,
    23	    >>>     decorrelate_dhdl)
    24	    >>> bz = load_benzene().data
    25	    >>> u_nk = extract_u_nk(bz['Coulomb'], T=300)
    26	    >>> decorrelated_u_nk = decorrelate_u_nk(u_nk, method='dhdl',
    27	    >>>     remove_burnin=True)
    28	    >>> dhdl = extract_dHdl(bz['Coulomb'], T=300)
    29	    >>> decorrelated_dhdl = decorrelate_dhdl(dhdl, remove_burnin=True)
    30	
    31	Low-level functions
    32	--------------------
    33	To decorrelate the data, in addition to the dataframe that contains the
    34	:ref:`dHdl <dHdl>` or :ref:`u_nk <u_nk>`, a :mod:`pandas.Series` is needed for
    35	the autocorrection analysis. The series could be generated with
    36	:func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.u_nk2series` or
    37	:func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.dhdl2series` and feed into
    38	:func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.statistical_inefficiency` or
    39	:func:`~alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.equilibrium_detection`. ::
    40	
    41	    >>> from alchemlyb.parsing.gmx import extract_u_nk, extract_dHdl
    42	    >>> from alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling import (u_nk2series,
    43	    >>>     dhdl2series, statistical_inefficiency, equilibrium_detection)
    44	    >>> bz = load_benzene().data
    45	    >>> u_nk = extract_u_nk(bz['Coulomb'], T=300)
    46	    >>> u_nk_series = u_nk2series(u_nk, method='dE')
    47	    >>> decorrelate_u_nk = statistical_inefficiency(u_nk, series=u_nk_series)
    48	    >>> decorrelate_u_nk = equilibrium_detection(u_nk, series=u_nk_series)
    49	    >>> dhdl = extract_dHdl(bz['Coulomb'], T=300)
    50	    >>> dhdl_series = dhdl2series(dhdl)
    51	    >>> decorrelate_dhdl = statistical_inefficiency(dhdl, series=dhdl_series)
    52	    >>> decorrelate_dhdl = equilibrium_detection(dhdl, series=dhdl_series)
    53	
    54	API Reference
    55	-------------
    56	.. autofunction:: alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.decorrelate_u_nk
    57	.. autofunction:: alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.decorrelate_dhdl
    58	.. autofunction:: alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.u_nk2series
    59	.. autofunction:: alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.dhdl2series
    60	.. autofunction:: alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.slicing
    61	.. autofunction:: alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.statistical_inefficiency
    62	.. autofunction:: alchemlyb.preprocessing.subsampling.equilibrium_detection
    63

"""
Script to reproduce the NAMD decorrelation issue.
"""

import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from alchemlyb.parsing.namd import extract_u_nk
from alchemlyb.preprocessing import decorrelate_u_nk, u_nk2series
from alchemtest.namd import load_tyr2ala

def test_namd_decorrelation_issue():
    """Test to reproduce the NAMD decorrelation issue."""
    
    print("Loading NAMD test data...")
    dataset = load_tyr2ala()
    
    # Load forward data
    forward_filenames = dataset['data']['forward']
    print(f"Found {len(forward_filenames)} forward files")
    
    # Extract u_nk data - this should create a DataFrame with mixed fep-lambda values
    u_nk = extract_u_nk(forward_filenames, T=300)
    print(f"Extracted u_nk shape: {u_nk.shape}")
    print(f"Index names: {u_nk.index.names}")
    
    # Show the distribution of fep-lambda values
    fep_lambda_counts = u_nk.index.get_level_values('fep-lambda').value_counts().sort_index()
    print(f"FEP lambda distribution:")
    for lambda_val, count in fep_lambda_counts.items():
        print(f"  fep-lambda={lambda_val}: {count} entries")
    
    # Show first few rows to understand structure
    print("\nFirst 10 rows:")
    print(u_nk.head(10))
    
    # Show column names
    print(f"\nColumn names: {list(u_nk.columns)}")
    
    # Test the u_nk2series function directly
    print("\n=== Testing u_nk2series function ===")
    try:
        series_all = u_nk2series(u_nk, method='all')
        print(f"Series (method='all') length: {len(series_all)}")
        print(f"Series (method='all') first 10 values:")
        print(series_all.head(10))
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error with method='all': {e}")
        
    try:
        series_de = u_nk2series(u_nk, method='dE')
        print(f"Series (method='dE') length: {len(series_de)}")
        print(f"Series (method='dE') first 10 values:")
        print(series_de.head(10))
        
        # Check if the series values correspond to the right columns for different fep-lambda values
        print("\nChecking series correspondence:")
        unique_lambdas = sorted(u_nk.index.get_level_values('fep-lambda').unique())
        print(f"Unique lambdas: {unique_lambdas}")
        
        for lambda_val in unique_lambdas[:3]:  # Check first 3 lambdas
            subset = u_nk[u_nk.index.get_level_values('fep-lambda') == lambda_val]
            subset_series = series_de[series_de.index.get_level_values('fep-lambda') == lambda_val]
            print(f"\nfep-lambda={lambda_val}:")
            print(f"  Subset shape: {subset.shape}")
            print(f"  Series length: {len(subset_series)}")
            
            # Show first few rows of subset and corresponding series values
            print(f"  First 3 rows of subset:")
            print(subset.head(3))
            print(f"  Corresponding series values:")
            print(subset_series.head(3))
            
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error with method='dE': {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
    
    # Test decorrelation
    print("\n=== Testing decorrelation ===")
    try:
        decorrelated = decorrelate_u_nk(u_nk, method='dE', drop_duplicates=True, sort=True)
        print(f"Decorrelated data shape: {decorrelated.shape}")
        
        # Check fep-lambda distribution in decorrelated data
        decorrelated_lambda_counts = decorrelated.index.get_level_values('fep-lambda').value_counts().sort_index()
        print(f"Decorrelated fep-lambda distribution:")
        for lambda_val, count in decorrelated_lambda_counts.items():
            print(f"  fep-lambda={lambda_val}: {count} entries")
            
        # This should show if we're losing data from certain fep-lambda values
        print("\nComparison of lambda distributions:")
        for lambda_val in fep_lambda_counts.index:
            original_count = fep_lambda_counts[lambda_val]
            decorrelated_count = decorrelated_lambda_counts.get(lambda_val, 0)
            ratio = decorrelated_count / original_count if original_count > 0 else 0
            print(f"  fep-lambda={lambda_val}: {original_count} -> {decorrelated_count} ({ratio:.2%})")
            
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error during decorrelation: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Perfect! I've reproduced the issue. Now I can clearly see what's happening:

1. **The core problem**: The `u_nk2series` function with `method='dE'` always selects column `0.05` (the second column) for ALL rows, regardless of what fep-lambda each row belongs to.

2. **Evidence**: 
   - For `fep-lambda=0.0`: The subset shows valid data in column `0.05`, and the series correctly extracts those values
   - For `fep-lambda=0.05`: The subset shows that column `0.05` contains all zeros, but the series still extracts from column `0.05` (which gives zeros)
   - For `fep-lambda=0.1`: The subset shows that column `0.05` contains NaN values, but the series still extracts from column `0.05` (which gives NaN)

3. **The IndexError**: This occurs because when the series contains many NaN values, the statistical inefficiency calculation fails or produces invalid indices.

This confirms the issue described by the user. The problem is that `u_nk2series` doesn't handle mixed fep-lambda DataFrames correctly. It should compute the appropriate energy difference for each row based on its own fep-lambda value, not use a single column for all rows.

Let me create a simpler test to demonstrate this more clearly:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/alchemistry__alchemlyb__1.0/simple_test.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Simple test to demonstrate the u_nk2series issue.
"""

import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from alchemlyb.preprocessing import u_nk2series

def test_u_nk2series_issue():
    """Demonstrate the u_nk2series issue with mixed fep-lambda data."""
    
    # Create a simple test DataFrame that mimics NAMD u_nk structure
    # Two fep-lambda values: 0.0 and 0.5
    # For fep-lambda=0.0, we should use column 0.5 (next column)  
    # For fep-lambda=0.5, we should use column 0.0 (previous column) or column 1.0 (next column)
    
    data = {
        'time': [1000.0, 1000.0, 2000.0, 2000.0],
        'fep-lambda': [0.0, 0.5, 0.0, 0.5],
        0.0: [0.0, 10.0, 0.0, 15.0],      # fep-lambda 0.0 data
        0.5: [5.0, 0.0, 8.0, 0.0],        # fep-lambda 0.5 data  
        1.0: [12.0, 3.0, 20.0, 7.0]       # Next column for fep-lambda 0.5
    }
    
    df = pd.DataFrame(data)
    df.set_index(['time', 'fep-lambda'], inplace=True)
    
    print("Test DataFrame:")
    print(df)
    print()
    
    print("Row by row analysis:")
    for idx, row in df.iterrows():
        time, lambda_val = idx
        print(f"time={time}, fep-lambda={lambda_val}")
        print(f"  Row data: {row.to_dict()}")
        if lambda_val == 0.0:
            # Should use column 0.5 (next lambda)
            correct_value = row[0.5]
            print(f"  Correct dE (using column 0.5): {correct_value}")
        elif lambda_val == 0.5:
            # Could use column 0.0 (previous) or 1.0 (next) - let's use next
            correct_value = row[1.0]
            print(f"  Correct dE (using column 1.0): {correct_value}")
    print()
    
    # Test current u_nk2series behavior
    print("Current u_nk2series behavior:")
    try:
        series = u_nk2series(df, method='dE')
        print("Generated series:")
        print(series)
        print()
        
        print("Analysis of current approach:")
        # The current approach uses the first row's lambda to determine column
        first_row_lambda = df.index[0][1]  # fep-lambda from first row
        print(f"First row fep-lambda: {first_row_lambda}")
        
        # It will consistently use the same column for all rows
        if first_row_lambda == 0.0:
            used_column = 0.5
            print(f"All rows use column {used_column} (next column for lambda 0.0)")
        elif first_row_lambda == 0.5:
            used_column = 1.0  # Assuming it picks the next column  
            print(f"All rows use column {used_column} (next column for lambda 0.5)")
            
        for idx, (_, row) in zip(df.index, df.iterrows()):
            time, lambda_val = idx
            series_value = series.loc[idx]
            used_value = row[used_column]
            print(f"  time={time}, fep-lambda={lambda_val}: series={series_value}, from_col_{used_column}={used_value}")
            
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()

1. **Correct values should be**:
   - fep-lambda=0.0: Use column 0.5 → 5.0, 8.0
